
```yaml

source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 18b

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Those raised as guardians must hold no private property — no gold, silver, or any other possession — but receive a measured wage for their guardianship from those they protect, spend it in common, live together, and devote themselves wholly to virtue."
greek_terms:
  - χρυσὸν
  - ἄργυρον
  - κτῆμα
  - ἐπικούρους
  - ἀρετῆς
```




```yaml

  - ἀναξίους
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0009
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 19b-19c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 19b-19c
  start_marker: 19b

  text_sha256: "a4ba5053a90e17f095025e974aa00556c28bcac045cfcb5d5ebdb6f28e6a8031"
speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The constitution described in the prior day's account is like a beautiful but motionless animal in a painting; Socrates desires to hear an account of that city in motion — competing in wars and contests against other cities, acting suitably in deeds and in words."
greek_terms:
  - ζῷα
  - γραφῆς
  - κινούμενά
  - ἀθλοῦντα
  - πόλιν
  - ἀγωνιζομένην

final_status: left_standing

limits: "This records Socrates' stated desire, not a doctrinal thesis. It does not establish that the city-in-motion account will be supplied; it is a request framed as a methodological desideratum."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0010
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 19d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 19d

speaker: ΣΩ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The imitative kind will imitate most easily and best whatever it has been reared in; what lies outside its rearing is harder to imitate well."
greek_terms:
  - μιμητικὸν
  - ἔθνος
  - ἐντραφῇ
  - μιμήσεται
  - τροφῆς

review_status: accepted
```


```yaml

content: "The elder Critias reported that the account concerned the greatest and most renowned deed that Athens performed, which was lost through time and the destruction of its doers."
greek_terms: ["περὶ μεγίστης καὶ ὀνομαστοτάσης πασῶν δικαιότατ’ ἂν πράξεως οὔσης", "ἣν ἥδε ἡ πόλις ἔπραξε μέν, διὰ δὲ χρόνον καὶ φθορὰν τῶν ἐργασαμένων οὐ διήρκεσε δεῦρο ὁ λόγος"]
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: "21d"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 21d
      start_marker: 21d
      end_marker: 21d
      start_char: 8526
      end_char: 9007
      text_sha256: "39730fd28c66bff8530f991e95521fd6ac6f5f9b7148ed08a5a59e431e759cc0"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "The report only characterizes the subject matter as Athens' greatest deed; it does not specify what the deed was or provide grounds for the superlative claim."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

claim_kind: report
content: "Solon, when questioning the most learned Egyptian priests about antiquity, discovered that neither he nor any other Greek knew practically anything about such ancient matters."
greek_terms: ["τὰ παλαιὰ ἀνερωτῶν", "σχεδὸν οὔτε αὑτὸν οὔτε ἄλλον Ἕλληνα οὐδένα οὐδὲν ὡς ἔπος εἰπεῖν εἰδότα περὶ τῶν τοιούτων ἀνευρεῖν"]
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0021
source_work: "Timaeus"

content: "The Phaethon story has the shape of myth, but the truth behind it is a deviation of celestial bodies that orbit the earth, occurring at long intervals and causing destruction of earthly things by great fire."
greek_terms: ["τοῦτο μύθου μὲν σχῆμα ἔχον λέγεται", "τὸ δὲ ἀληθές ἐστι τῶν περὶ γῆν κατ᾽ οὐρανὸν ἰόντων παράλλαξις"]

limits: "Does not establish the astronomical mechanics of the deviation, nor does it confirm that the Phaethon myth is solely explicable this way. The claim is asserted by the Egyptian priest, not independently argued."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0023
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "23a-23b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 23a-23b
  start_marker: 23a
  end_marker: 23b
  start_char: 11552
  end_char: 12450
  text_sha256: "a6fc9c9893f7585491305e0bd426e46335fc255022e51a5948faaaf8b72321ec"
speaker: "ΚΡ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "In Egypt, all noble or great deeds and everything else of note has been recorded from ancient times and preserved in the temples, whereas among the Greeks and others such records are newly established and periodically destroyed by celestial floods, leaving them illiterate and unmusical."
greek_terms: ["πάντα γεγραμμένα ἐκ παλαιοῦ τῇδ᾽ ἐστὶν ἐν τοῖς ἱεροῖς καὶ σεσωσμένα", "ὥσπερ νόσημα ἥκει φερόμενον αὐτοῖς ῥεῦμα οὐράνιον καὶ τοὺς ἀγραμμάτους τε καὶ ἀμούσους ἔλιπεν ὑμῶν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "23a-23b"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 23a-23b
      start_marker: 23a
      end_marker: 23b
      start_char: 11552
      end_char: 12450
      text_sha256: "a6fc9c9893f7585491305e0bd426e46335fc255022e51a5948faaaf8b72321ec"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Does not establish the actual completeness or accuracy of Egyptian temple records, nor does it prove that all Greek records are wiped out in every cycle. The claim serves the Egyptian priest's explanation of why Greeks lack ancient memory."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0024
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "23b-23c"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

content: "The Greeks remember only one flood, though many have occurred before, and they are unaware that the finest and best race of men once existed in their land, from which Solon and the present city are descended from a small surviving seed."
greek_terms: ["ἕνα γῆς κατακλυσμὸν μέμνησθε πολλῶν ἔμπροσθεν γεγονότων", "τὸ κάλλιστον καὶ ἄριστον γένος ἐπ᾽ ἀνθρώπους ἐν τῇ χώρᾳ παρ᾽ ὑμῖν οὐκ ἴστε γεγονός"]

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0025
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "23c-23d"

content: "Before the greatest destruction by water, the city that is now Athens was the best in war and most excellently governed in every respect, and the finest deeds and constitutions are said to have arisen there."
greek_terms: ["ἡ νῦν Ἀθηναίων οὖσα πόλις ἀρίστη πρός τε τὸν πόλεμον καὶ κατὰ πάντα εὐνομωτάτη διαφερόντως"]

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0026
source_work: "Timaeus"

speaker: "ΚΡ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The Athenian polity was established a thousand years before the Egyptian one, with Athena receiving the seed of Athens from Earth and Hephaestus; the Egyptian order is recorded in the sacred writings as eight thousand years old."
greek_terms: ["προτέραν μὲν τὴν παρ᾽ ὑμῖν ἔτεσιν χιλίοις, ἐκ Γῆς τε καὶ Ἡφαίστου τὸ σπέρμα παραλαβοῦσα ὑμῶν", "τῆς δὲ ἐνθάδε διακοσμήσεως παρ᾽ ἡμῖν ἐν τοῖς ἱεροῖς γράμμασιν ὀκτακισχιλίων ἐτῶν ἀριθμὸς γέγραπται"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "23e-24a"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 23e-24a

      end_char: 13980
      text_sha256: "31298d6a48c98148ce7e0247fd5363a2375d3430b1d1668f76d98b3b5764717b"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Does not establish the chronological precision of these figures; they are reported by the Egyptian priest and depend on temple-record authority. The 9000-year interval (the ancient Athenians from whom deeds will be narrated) is flagged but set aside for later detailed treatment."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0027
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "24a-24b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 24a-24b

speaker: "ΚΡ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "In ancient Egypt, the class of priests was set apart from the others, then the class of craftsmen — each craft working by itself without mixing with others — and likewise the class of herdsmen, hunters, and farmers."
greek_terms: ["πρῶτον μὲν τὸ τῶν ἱερέων γένος ἀπὸ τῶν ἄλλων χωρὶς ἀφωρισμένον", "μετὰ δὲ τοῦτο τὸ τῶν δημιουργῶν, ὅτι καθ᾽ αὑτὸ ἕκαστον ἄλλῳ δὲ οὐκ ἐπιμειγνύμενον δημιουργεῖ"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "24a-24b"

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Does not establish that the ancient Athenian class structure was identical; the Egyptian priest offers the Egyptian arrangement as a parallel for Solon to compare with Athenian laws. The record reports only what the priest claims about Egyptian class separation."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0030
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 24d-24e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 24d-24e
  start_marker: 24d
  end_marker: 24e
  start_char: 14834
  end_char: 15683
  text_sha256: "87f4a5cff78e311a91d42601ea2123dcb857e4615a09e15a23fc81968e244af9"
speaker: ΚΡ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The greatest deed of ancient Athens, surpassing all others in magnitude and virtue, was stopping a power that was advancing in insolence against all Europe and Asia from outside, setting out from the Atlantic sea.
greek_terms:
  - ὕβρει
  - Ἀτλαντικοῦ πελάγους
stance_events:
  - kind: reported
    stephanus_span: 24d-24e
    source_ref:
```






```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0044
```


```yaml

speaker: "ΤΙ."
```

```yaml
content: "Accounts are akin to that which they expound: accounts of what is stable and certain should themselves be stable and irrefutable, while accounts of what is an image should be likely accounts, for as being is to becoming, so truth is to trust."
greek_terms: ["εἰκότας", "ὅτιπερ πρὸς γένεσιν οὐσία, τοῦτο πρὸς πίστιν ἀλήθεια"]
```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0066
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "36b-36d"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The demiurge split the whole constructed soul-structure lengthwise into two, bent each into a circle, joined them at their midpoints and at the point opposite the joining, and set them in motion: the outer circle was named the circle of the Same, moving to the right along the side, and the inner circle was named the circle of the Different, moving to the left along the diagonal."
greek_terms: ["ταὐτοῦ", "θατέρου", "κύκλῳ", "ἐπὶ δεξιὰ", "ἐπ' ἀριστερά"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "36b-36d"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 36b-36d

      end_char: 33620
      text_sha256: "1e49e66dfdb037fd3298e828cc3c3b5009fbd63f264d612a63087ebdcafe7f61"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish whether the circles are literal celestial orbits or geometric models, nor does it specify which circle corresponds to the fixed stars and which to the planets."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0069
source_work: "Timaeus"
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0073
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "37e"

content: "Days, nights, months, and years — which did not exist before the heaven came to be — were contrived at the same time as the heaven's composition; these are all parts of time, and 'was' and 'will be' are generated forms of time which we wrongly transfer to eternal being."
greek_terms: ["ἡμέρας", "νύκτας", "μῆνας", "ἐνιαυτούς", "χρόνου", "οὐσίαν"]

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0075
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0081

  The Demiurge returns to the mixing bowl used for the world soul and mixes
  the remaining material in the same manner, but the mixture is no longer
  purely uniform — it is second and third in purity.

  - κρατῆρα
  - δεύτερα
```

```yaml
  The Demiurge declares a law of reincarnation: if souls master the
  affections they live in justice; if mastered, in injustice; one who lives
  well returns to his companion star for a blessed life; failing, he is
  reborn into a woman's nature in the second birth; if evil persists, he
  transforms into a beastly nature corresponding to his mode of vice, and
  does not cease from toils until he masters the irrational mass with reason
  and reaches the form of the first and best condition.
greek_terms:

  - ἀδικίᾳ
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0088

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: >-
  The young gods, imitating their own Demiurge, borrow portions of fire,
  earth, water, and air from the cosmos — to be returned later — and
  fabricate each mortal body as a single composite, binding the revolutions
  of the immortal soul into a body subject to influx and efflux; the bound
  soul-revolutions neither master nor are mastered but are carried by force,
  causing the whole living being to move with all six motions in a disorderly
  and irrational manner.
greek_terms:
  - δανειζόμενοι
  - ἀποδοθησόμενα
  - γόμφοις
  - ἐπίρρυτον
  - ἀπόρρυτον
  - ἓξ κινήσεις

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: >-
  The record reports Timaeus's account of the young gods' demiurgy and the
  initial disorder of embodied souls; it does not establish that the six
  motions exhaust all corporeal motions or that the borrowing-and-return
  schema is defended elsewhere.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0089
source_work: Timaeus

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: >-
  The violent motions carried through the body from external collisions —
  fire, solid earth, liquid water, or wind — strike the soul and are
  therefore called, then and now, sense-perceptions collectively; together
  with the continuous flowing channel they shake the soul's revolutions so
  violently that the revolution of the Same is entirely bound and halted
  while the revolution of the Different is disrupted, distorting the
  harmonic intervals of double, triple, hemiolic, epitritic, and epogdoic
  ratios whose bonds only the one who bound them can dissolve.
greek_terms:
  - αἰσθήσεις
  - ταὐτοῦ
  - θατέρου
  - διπλασίου
  - τριπλασίου
  - ἡμιολίων
  - ἐπιτρίτων
  - ἐπογδόων

    stephanus_span: 43c-43d

      end_char: 47974
      text_sha256: "676a522c1ac4a6e1394e3d40d298d84da4cdbb395d97de0a6e88eaee1d58f870"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: >-
  The record captures Timaeus's account of how embodiment disrupts the
  rational soul-structure; it does not assert that the harmonic ratios are
  demonstrated within this span or that the revolution of the Same is
  permanently disabled.
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0093

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: Sight is the cause of the greatest benefit to us, because none of the present discourse about the universe would ever have been spoken if we had not seen stars, sun, or heaven; day, night, months, yearly cycles, equinoxes, and solstices have produced number, the notion of time, and inquiry into the nature of the whole, from which we have acquired philosophy.
greek_terms:
  - ὄψις
  - φιλοσοφίας
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 46e-47b
    source_ref:

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0094

content: The god discovered and gave us sight so that by observing the circuits of intellect in the heaven we might use them for the revolutions of our own thought, which are akin to them, and by learning and sharing in the correctness of calculations according to nature we might stabilize the wandering revolutions within us by imitating the entirely unwandering revolutions of the god.
greek_terms:
  - νοῦ
  - περιόδους
  - διανοήσεως
  - ἀπλανεῖς

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

claim_id: claim_timaeus_0097
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 48a-48b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 48a-48b
  start_marker: 48a
  end_marker: 48b
  start_char: 56442
  end_char: 57278
  text_sha256: "2fbeac5e1fe96945ae2693c3e8ddd5079ae2f7ae641c685436b5a10a9bc5d695"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: The generation of this cosmos was produced as a mixture of necessity and intellect, with intellect ruling necessity by persuading it to lead most of the things that come to be toward the best, and the whole was originally constituted in this way, through necessity yielding to intelligent persuasion.
greek_terms:
  - ἀνάγκης
  - νοῦ
  - πειθοῦς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 48a-48b
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 48a-48b
      start_marker: 48a
      end_marker: 48b
      start_char: 56442
      end_char: 57278
      text_sha256: "2fbeac5e1fe96945ae2693c3e8ddd5079ae2f7ae641c685436b5a10a9bc5d695"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: This record does not specify the mechanism by which intellect persuades necessity, nor does it specify which things that come to be are not led toward the best.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0098
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 48b-48c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 48b-48c
  start_marker: 48b
  end_marker: 48c
  start_char: 56835
  end_char: 57682
  text_sha256: "fd889017a7b62ea03beb8c375c0f1d5a6c6e4b14e8573a8480aa77d716488301"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: No one has yet revealed the generation of fire, water, air, and earth; people speak as though they knew what fire and each of them is, positing them as principles and elements of the whole, when it is not even reasonable to liken them to the forms of syllables.
greek_terms:
  - στοιχεῖα
  - συλλαβῆς
  - ἀρχὰς
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 48b-48c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 48b-48c
      start_marker: 48b
      end_marker: 48c
      start_char: 56835
      end_char: 57682
      text_sha256: "fd889017a7b62ea03beb8c375c0f1d5a6c6e4b14e8573a8480aa77d716488301"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: This record does not establish what the true generation or nature of the four so-called elements is; it raises a deficiency in previous accounts and sets up the need for a new starting point.
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0104
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 49b-49d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 49b-49d
  start_marker: 49b

  text_sha256: "d8c14bdec196316f0545a8be25f15582fe446235888058dd67c67b6996ffff5a"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: method_rule
content: One should not confidently assert of any observable elemental thing (water, fire, air, etc.) that it is this and not another, because they all transform in a cycle into one another and never appear the same.
greek_terms: []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 49d
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 49d
      start_marker: 49d

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Restricts the use of stable identity language for changing phenomena; does not deny that the cycle of transformation occurs."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0105
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 49d-49e

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: For what is always seen becoming different at different times (e.g., fire), the safest speech is to call it "suchlike" rather than "this", because it flees and does not abide any designation implying permanence.

  - τόδε
  - τοῦτο
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0107
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 50a-50b
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0111
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 50d-50e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 50d-50e
  start_marker: 50d

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: That which is to receive all kinds in itself must be outside all forms, just as fragrant ointments require an odorless liquid base — otherwise its own features would interfere with and distort the impressions it receives.
greek_terms:
  - ἐκτὸς εἰδῶν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 50e
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Argues for the formlessness requirement via analogy with ointments and molding; does not claim that the receptacle is itself a spatial void."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Before proceeding further, one must investigate whether there is a fire itself by itself and whether each of the things we always speak of thus exists itself by itself."
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0116
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "51c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The alternatives are: either the things we see and perceive through the body are the only things with truth, and there is no intelligible form of each thing — in which case talk of forms is empty — or intelligible forms exist."
greek_terms: ["εἶδος ἑκάστου νοητόν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: "51c"
    source_ref:

final_status: posed_only

limits: "This record does not establish which alternative Timaeus endorses here; the question is presented as a dichotomy to be decided."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0117
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "51d-51e"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "If intellection and true opinion are two distinct kinds, then there exist forms that are themselves by themselves, imperceptible to us and grasped only by intellection."
greek_terms: ["νοῦς", "δόξα ἀληθής", "εἴδη", "νοούμενα μόνον"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "51d-51e"

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0118
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "51e"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 51e
  start_marker: 51e
  end_marker: 51e

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Intellection and true opinion are two distinct kinds because they come about separately and are unlike: intellection arises through teaching with true account and is immovable by persuasion, while opinion arises through persuasion, is irrational, and is persuadable."
greek_terms: ["διδαχῆς", "πειθοῦς", "ἀληθοῦς λόγου", "ἄλογον", "ἀκίνητον πειθοῖ", "μεταπειστόν"]

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that all humans lack intellection; Timaeus says intellection belongs to gods and a small class of humans."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0119
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "52a"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

claim_kind: thesis
content: "There is a first kind: the form, which is self-identical, ungenerated, imperishable, neither receiving anything else into itself nor entering into another, invisible and imperceptible, the object of intellection."
greek_terms: ["κατὰ ταὐτὰ εἶδος", "ἀγέννητον", "ἀνώλεθρον", "νόησις"]

  - kind: asserted

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0120
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "52a"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

claim_kind: thesis
content: "There is a second kind, homonymous with and similar to the form: the perceptible, generated, always in motion, coming to be in some place and perishing from it, graspable by opinion with perception."
greek_terms: ["ὁμώνυμον", "αἰσθητόν", "γεννητόν", "πεφορημένον", "δόξῃ μετ’ αἰσθήσεως"]
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0121
source_work: "Timaeus"

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0122
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "52b-52c"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "We are like dreamers who say that everything that is must be somewhere in some place occupying some space, and that what is neither on earth nor in heaven is nothing; under this dreamlike state we cannot wake to distinguish the truth."
greek_terms: ["ὀνειροπολοῦμεν", "ὀνειρώξεως"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "52b-52c"
    source_ref:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that the dreamlike state is inescapable; Timaeus presents the argument that follows as an awakening from it."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0123
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "52c-52d"

claim_kind: thesis
content: "An image, since it is not its own and is a phantom of something else that always moves, must come to be in something other than itself, clinging to being in some way, or else be nothing at all."
greek_terms: ["εἰκόνι", "φάντασμα", "ἑτέρῳ"]

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0125
source_work: timaeus
stephanus_span: 53c-53d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 53c-53d
  start_marker: 53c
  end_marker: 53d
  start_char: 67452
  end_char: 68259
  text_sha256: "de0b262ecd390f1d85c8dbc6d8cc9f375c5f50f2bd3b64abeea6d51522c1a0d5"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Fire, earth, water, and air are bodies; every bodily form has depth; depth necessarily encompasses plane surface nature; plane surfaces are constituted from right-angled triangles; all triangles originate from two kinds of right-angled triangles — isosceles and scalene."
greek_terms:
  - σώματά
  - βάθος
  - ἐπίπεδον
  - τρίγωνα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 53c-53d
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 53c-53d

      end_char: 68259
      text_sha256: "de0b262ecd390f1d85c8dbc6d8cc9f375c5f50f2bd3b64abeea6d51522c1a0d5"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "States the geometric reduction thesis but does not establish why these two triangles in particular are elemental or whether this represents physical reality or a likely account."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0127
source_work: timaeus
stephanus_span: 54a-54b
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 54a-54b

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Of the two right-angled triangles, the isosceles has one nature while the scalene has unlimited natures; for constructing the elements, the finest scalene triangle is selected — the one whose greater side is triple in power the lesser side, i.e. the half-equilateral triangle."
greek_terms:
  - ἰσοσκελὲς
  - πρόμηκες
  - τριπλῆν κατὰ δύναμιν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 54a-54b

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "The selection of the half-equilateral as finest is posited rather than demonstrated; Timaeus allows that someone else might select a finer triangle and win as a friend (54a)."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0128
source_work: timaeus
stephanus_span: 54b-54d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 54b-54d
  start_marker: 54b
  end_marker: 54d

  end_char: 70429
  text_sha256: "8150404d7ac2003d036ec954f2ce0b5b1ab89b7274d840b88b79a7fe1b33d238"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Three of the four bodily kinds — fire, air, and water — are generated from the scalene triangle and can be dissolved into one another, changing from many small to few large and vice versa; the fourth kind alone, earth, is generated from the isosceles triangle and cannot be dissolved into the other three."
greek_terms:
  - γένη τέτταρα
  - ἰσοσκελοῦς τριγώνου
  - διαλυόμενα
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 54b-54d
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 54b-54d

      end_char: 70429
      text_sha256: "8150404d7ac2003d036ec954f2ce0b5b1ab89b7274d840b88b79a7fe1b33d238"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "The claim corrects an earlier appearance (54b) that all four kinds appeared to intertransform; it does not assign specific solids to fire, air, or water or provide empirical evidence for intertransformability."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0129
source_work: timaeus
stephanus_span: 54e-55c

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

claim_kind: thesis
content: "Four regular solids are constructed geometrically: the first and smallest (pyramid) from four equilateral triangles, the second (octahedron) from eight, the third (icosahedron) from twenty, and the fourth (cube) from six squares composed of isosceles right triangles."
greek_terms:
  - ἰσόπλευρον τρίγωνον
  - στερεὰν γωνίαν
  - κυβικόν
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "States the geometric construction of the four solids but does not yet assign them to specific elements except that the cube is assigned to earth at 55d; does not prove that these are the only possible regular solids."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

limits: "Record does not establish that Timaeus has empirically verified these geometric claims; the argument rests on the prior hypothesis of the two original triangles and the eikōs logos method."
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0137

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When earth encounters fire it is dissolved and carried about, but its parts can recombine to become earth again; earth never changes into another kind. Water divided by fire or air can recombine as one fire-body and two air-bodies; and air segments from one dissolved part become two fire-bodies. Conversely, when a small amount of fire is surrounded and overpowered, two fire-bodies combine into one air-form, and when air is mastered and fragmented, from two whole and one half parts one whole water-form is compacted."
greek_terms: ["γῆ", "πυρί", "διαλυθεῖσά", "ὕδωρ", "ἀέρος"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "56d-56e"

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Record does not establish that these transformations are empirically observed; they follow from the geometric theory of the primary bodies and the eikōs logos."
review_status: accepted
```




```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0143
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "57e-58a"

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Motion can never arise in uniformity, because it is difficult, or rather impossible, for what is to be moved to exist without a mover, or a mover without what is to be moved; and these cannot be uniform. Therefore rest is set in uniformity and motion in non-uniformity, and the cause of non-uniform nature is inequality."
greek_terms: ["ὁμαλότητι", "κίνησιν", "κινησόμενον", "κινήσοντος", "ἀνωμαλότητα", "ἀνισότης"]

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Record does not establish that motion and rest are fully explained; the origin of inequality, though said to have been discussed, is not demonstrated here, and the mover-moved requirement is treated as a difficulty rather than proved."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
content: "Water divides into two primary kinds: the liquid kind, which is mobile due to its small and unequal constituent particles, and the fusible kind, which is more stable and heavy because it is composed of large, uniform particles but becomes mobile when fire dissolves its uniformity."
greek_terms: []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "58d-58e"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 58d-58e
      start_marker: 58d
      end_marker: 58e
      start_char: 78157
      end_char: 79023
      text_sha256: "dcb84be4ca1112a87086845d781f43dff9de4ca0fafee12555cbdeab48774f0e"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that the liquid/fusible division is exhaustive for water; it only notes two primary kinds and the mechanism of mobility via fire."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When fire departs from a fusible body, the surrounding air compresses the still-mobile mass back into the fire's former seats, restoring its uniformity; the departure of fire is called cooling, and the resulting consolidation after fire leaves is called freezing."
greek_terms: []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "58e-59a"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that cooling and freezing are defined for all bodies; the account is given specifically for the fusible water-kind."
review_status: accepted
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0154

content: "Most water-kinds mixed together and filtered through earth-plants are called juices; among the many nameless varieties, four fire-containing kinds with names are distinguished: wine, which heats soul with body; the oily kind including pitch, castor-oil, and olive oil, which is smooth and distinguishes vision; honey, which produces sweetness by dissolving the mouth's natural convergence; and the frothy kind called opos, which dissolves flesh by burning."
greek_terms: ["ὀπός"]
stance_events:

    stephanus_span: "60a-60b"
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0164
source_work: "Timaeus"

content: "The affection called cold arises when larger moist particles entering the body expel smaller ones and, unable to enter their seats, compress the body's moisture, producing immobility through uniformity and condensation; the unnatural compression fights back, and the resulting tremor and shivering is called cold."
greek_terms: ["ῥῖγος"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "62a-62b"
    source_ref:
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0165
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "62b-62c"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 62b-62c

claim_kind: thesis
content: "Hard is whatever our flesh yields to; soft is whatever yields to our flesh; yielding depends on a small base, while that which is composed of square bases, being firmly fixed, is most resistant, and what is compressed into the greatest density is most unyielding."
greek_terms: []
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "62b-62c"

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Defines hard and soft relative to flesh-yielding; relies on the earlier geometric theory of elemental shapes (square bases for earth). Does not address absolute hardness independent of flesh."
```







```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0184
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "68d"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_marker: 68d

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Anyone who attempted to test these colour-mixtures by experiment would be ignorant of the difference between human and divine nature, because god alone is able to blend many into one and dissolve one into many, while no human is now or ever will be capable of either."
greek_terms: ["τὸ τῆς ἀνθρωπίνης καὶ θείας φύσεως ἠγνοηκὼς ἂν εἴη διάφορον", "θεὸς μὲν τὰ πολλὰ εἰς ἓν συγκεραννύναι καὶ πάλιν ἐξ ἑνὸς εἰς πολλὰ διαλύειν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "68d"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 68d
      start_marker: 68d
      end_marker: 68d

      end_char: 99028
      text_sha256: "e6e3bc62a1d409615db795b27d5e487067524a695d5a9299ea983a5c0dbcb41a"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that empirical testing is generally rejected; it only notes the specific claim about the impossibility of experimental verification of colour-mixtures in relation to the human-divine distinction."
review_status: accepted
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0186
source_work: "Timaeus"

review_status: accepted
```





```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0192
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0194

stephanus_span: 70a

content: "The part of the soul that partakes of courage and spiritedness, being victory-loving, was settled nearer the head between the midriff and neck, so that it might hear reason and together with reason forcibly restrain the appetitive kind."

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0195
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0198
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0199
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 71b-71c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 71b-71c
  start_marker: 71b
  end_marker: 71c
  start_char: 103945
  end_char: 104776
  text_sha256: "b1ed03203b94996abba7267e82064baaaf9dc3d3b5b63f9af66f3a28dbd93e2a"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The liver receives the power of thoughts coming from intellect as a mirror receiving impressions; using its bitterness, it threatens and frightens the appetitive soul, producing bile-colors, wrinkling, and roughness, causing pains and distress."
greek_terms: [ἥπατος, κατόπτρῳ, τύπους, εἴδωλα, πικρότητος, χολώδη]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 71b-71c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 71b-71c
      start_marker: 71b
      end_marker: 71c
      start_char: 103945
      end_char: 104776
      text_sha256: "b1ed03203b94996abba7267e82064baaaf9dc3d3b5b63f9af66f3a28dbd93e2a"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This records the mirror mechanism of the liver in its frightening function; it does not establish that this is the only means by which intellect communicates with the appetitive part."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0200
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 71c-71d
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 71c-71d
  start_marker: 71c

speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Conversely, when gentle inspiration from thought paints contrary images, the liver's bitterness rests and its natural sweetness straightens it all smooth and free, making the liver-dwelling soul-part cheerful and calm, enabling measured nighttime conduct and divination through sleep."
greek_terms: [πρᾳότητος, ἐπίπνοια, γλυκύτητι, ἵλεων, εὐήμερον, μαντείᾳ]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This records the calming function of the liver and its connection to sleep divination; it does not establish that all dreams are divinatory or that the mechanism always produces reliable prophecy."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0201
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 71e
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

claim_kind: thesis
content: "God gave mantic skill to human folly: no one in his right mind attains inspired and true divination, but only when the power of thought is fettered in sleep, or displaced by disease or some divine enthusiasm."
greek_terms: [μαντικὴν, ἀφροσύνῃ, ἔννους, ἐνθέου, ἀληθοῦς, φρονήσεως, πεδηθεὶς, ἐνθουσιασμὸν]

```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0203
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "74e"

greek_terms: ["ἐμψυχότατα", "ἀψυχότατα", "σάρκα"]

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "74e"

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0204
source_work: "Timaeus"

claim_kind: thesis
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0206
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "75c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 75c
  start_marker: 75c
  end_marker: 75c

  text_sha256: "0f590bfc842e6ff66f5b40fe0309978f1ce0eae293a971852789d2e3d62f401d"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: report
content: "The craftsmen of our generation, reasoning whether to produce a longer-lived inferior race or a shorter-lived better one, decided that the shorter better life is in every way to be chosen over the longer inferior one."
greek_terms: ["δημιουργοῖς", "πολυχρονιώτερον", "βραχυχρονιώτερον", "βέλτιον", "ἀμείνονα"]
stance_events:

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This is a reported decision attributed to the craftsmen or ordering gods, not a doctrine Timaeus asserts in his own voice; the report is offered as explanation for why the head is not sheathed in dense flesh."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0207
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "75c"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

content: "Because of that decision, the head was not roofed over with dense bone and flesh and sinew — since it has no joints — and as a result the head attached to the body is more sensitive and more intelligent but much weaker than the rest."

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0208
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "75d-75e"

content: "The power of the mouth — teeth, tongue, and lips — was arranged by the ordering gods for two purposes: the entrance of necessary things (nourishment for the body) and the exit of the best things (the stream of speech that serves intelligence)."
greek_terms: ["στόματος", "ὀδοῦσιν", "γλώττῃ", "χείλεσιν", "ἀναγκαίων", "ἀρίστων", "λόγων", "νᾶμα", "φρονήσει"]

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0209
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "75e-76a"
source_ref:

content: "The head could not be left as bare bone because of seasonal extremes, nor could it be buried in flesh making it deaf and insensate; instead the skin was formed as a covering that could be separated off from the drying fleshlike substance."
greek_terms: ["κεφαλὴν", "ὀστεΐνην", "δέρμα"]

  - kind: asserted

```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0210
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "76a-76c"

greek_terms: ["τριχῶν", "δέρματι", "πυρὶ", "ψύξεως", "ἱμαντῶδες"]
```


```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0212
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "76e-77a"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 76e-77a

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When the mortal creature's parts were complete, the gods contrived a remedy for its decay—plants, which are a kind of living being akin to human nature but formed with other shapes and senses."
greek_terms: ["φυτεύουσιν", "ἕτερον ζῷον", "δένδρα", "φυτὰ", "σπέρματα"]

  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "76e-77a"

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0213
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "77a-77c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 77a-77c
  start_marker: 77a
  end_marker: 77c

  text_sha256: "88754fbc3ff9a6a1243853bcc06d1f5f3d342709bce71081402a08c5c8865fc3"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Whatever partakes of life is most correctly called a living being, and plants partake of the third kind of soul—the appetitive kind located between the midriff and the navel—which has no share in opinion, reasoning, or intellect, but only in pleasant and painful sensation with desires."
greek_terms: ["ζῷον", "ψυχῆς εἴδους", "δόξης", "λογισμοῦ", "νοῦ", "αἰσθήσεως", "ἐπιθυμιῶν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "77a-77c"
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 77a-77c
      start_marker: 77a

      end_char: 117285
      text_sha256: "88754fbc3ff9a6a1243853bcc06d1f5f3d342709bce71081402a08c5c8865fc3"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record does not establish that the tripartite soul classification is identical to that in the Republic; the appetitive soul here is attributed to plants only. The record does not claim that plants possess the spirited or rational parts."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

  text_sha256: "e9d292ea31718edcdddeaa4244fc501a468111c79a5c4548e0c5a23e2f9cd151"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: method_rule
content: "Everything composed of smaller parts contains larger things, but things composed of larger parts cannot contain smaller ones; fire, having the smallest parts, can pass through everything and nothing can contain it."
greek_terms: ["στέγει", "πῦρ", "σμικρομερέστατον"]
stance_events:
  - kind: posed_for_examination
    stephanus_span: "78a-78b"
    source_ref:

      stephanus_span: 78a-78b

      end_char: 118897
      text_sha256: "e9d292ea31718edcdddeaa4244fc501a468111c79a5c4548e0c5a23e2f9cd151"
final_status: posed_only
observation_ids: []
limits: "This is a general physical principle stated as a premise for the subsequent account of respiration and digestion, not a conclusion from empirical demonstration. Its status as a thesis about material composition is recorded, not its truth."
review_status: accepted
```



```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0218
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "79a-79c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 79a-79c

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Respiration operates by circular displacement: because there is no void, expelled breath pushes adjacent air, which in turn displaces its neighbor, creating a continuous circuit that returns air to the vacated region."
greek_terms: ["κενὸν οὐδέν ἐστιν", "οὐκ εἰς κενόν", "περιελαυνόμενον", "οἷον τροχοῦ περιαγομένου"]

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim asserts a physical mechanism for respiration based on the denial of void; it does not establish that Timaeus offers independent empirical evidence for the mechanism beyond the circular-displacement model itself."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0219
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "79c-79e"

  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The ultimate cause of respiration is heat: the internal heat of living bodies, located around the blood and veins as an innate source of fire, moves naturally toward its own region, and the alternating displacement through the two exits (the bodily pores and the mouth/nostrils) produces the cycle of inhalation and exhalation."
greek_terms: ["πᾶν ζῷον αὑτοῦ τἀντὸς περὶ τὸ αἷμα καὶ τὰς φλέβας θερμότατα ἔχει", "οἷον ἐν ἑαυτῷ πηγήν τινα ἐνοῦσαν πυρός", "τὸ θερμὸν δὴ κατὰ φύσιν εἰς τὴν αὑτοῦ χώραν ἔξω πρὸς τὸ συγγενὲς ὁμολογητέον ἰέναι"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "79c-79e"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 79c-79e

      end_marker: 79e

      end_char: 122248
      text_sha256: "f3642dc8c22ddb131b7f06b6a925988b09722ff3a62fa4136280b86d4e783fdc"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim identifies the heat-difference mechanism as the cause that sets the circular-displacement respiration process in motion; it does not establish that this thermal account is empirically verified or that other causes are excluded."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0220
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "80a-80b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Harmony and discord in sounds arise from the similarity or dissimilarity of motions they produce in us: consonant sounds result when slower motions catch up with and blend seamlessly with faster motions as the latter die away, producing a single blended affection, while discord arises from dissimilarity of the motions."
greek_terms: ["ἀνάρμοστοι", "σύμφωνοι", "μίαν ἐξ ὀξείας καὶ βαρείας συνεκεράσαντο πάθην"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim states a physical account of consonance as motion-blending, with pleasure for the foolish and gladness for the wise arising from imitation of divine harmony; it does not establish a mathematical or ratio-based theory of musical intervals."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0221
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "80b-80c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 80b-80c
  start_marker: 80b
  end_marker: 80c
  start_char: 122647

  text_sha256: "b19d30ca5c1a988a05146ba7f9d4a401255520e3d0b89a450824af4c1a72fd93"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "There is no such thing as attraction: all phenomena attributed to attraction—including water currents, thunderbolts, amber, and the Heraclean stone—are explained by the absence of void and by things pushing one another circularly, each moving toward its own region as they separate and combine."
greek_terms: ["ὁλκὴ μὲν οὐκ ἔστιν οὐδενί ποτε", "τὸ δὲ κενὸν εἶναι μηδὲν", "περιωθεῖν τε αὑτὰ ταῦτα εἰς ἄλληλα"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "80b-80c"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 80b-80c
      start_marker: 80b
      end_marker: 80c
      start_char: 122647
      end_char: 123496
      text_sha256: "b19d30ca5c1a988a05146ba7f9d4a401255520e3d0b89a450824af4c1a72fd93"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim extends the no-void circular-displacement principle to reject attraction as a distinct causal mechanism; it does not establish that this account correctly explains magnetism or electrostatic phenomena in particular."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0222
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "80d-80e"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The nourishment of the whole body occurs through the cutting of food by internal fire and its distribution via the veins: fire divides the food, the divided particles are carried upward with the breath, and the veins draw this nourishment from the belly, producing the streaming flow of nutriment throughout all living bodies."
greek_terms: ["τέμνοντος μὲν τὰ σιτία τοῦ πυρός", "αἰωρουμένου δὲ ἐντὸς τῷ πνεύματι συνεπομένου", "τὰ τῆς τροφῆς νάματα"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "80d-80e"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 80d-80e
      start_marker: 80d
      end_marker: 80e

      end_char: 124298
      text_sha256: "b8d576a7b7863a74365c147e15540e6e942d6f06734555fe2aa58da040e2a274"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim recapitulates and extends the account of nutrition by linking fire-based cutting, pneumatic conveyance, and venous distribution; it does not establish that a separate digestive faculty or organ-specific processing is required beyond this mechanism."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0223
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "81a-81b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Growth and decay in living bodies follow the principle of like-to-like motion: each divided internal particle moves toward its own kind, replenishing what has been emptied, so that the body grows when inflow exceeds outflow and wastes when outflow exceeds inflow."
greek_terms: ["τὸ συγγενὲς πᾶν φέρεται πρὸς ἑαυτό", "ὅταν μὲν δὴ πλέον τοῦ ἐπιρρέοντος ἀπίῃ, φθίνει πᾶν, ὅταν δὲ ἔλαττον, αὐξάνεται"]

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This claim states a general principle of growth and decay as a balance of inflow and outflow driven by like-to-like affinity; it does not establish a quantitative account of metabolic rates or the role of specific organs in regulating growth."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0224
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "83e-84b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 83e-84b
  start_marker: 83e
  end_marker: 84b
  start_char: 130074
  end_char: 131248
  text_sha256: "627ba5ab7ce230abcb0027f80abced95443bb9bfea07630f56d1831bb197282f"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When flesh is decomposed by disease but its foundations remain, the power of the affliction is halved, since recovery is still easy; but when the bond connecting flesh to bone falls sick and becomes rough, salty, and desiccated rather than remaining the nourishing-dew binding, it detaches from bone, leaving nerves bare and full of brine, and these detached substances re-enter the blood-stream and multiply the previously described diseases."
greek_terms: ["ἡμίσεια τῆς συμφορᾶς ἡ δύναμις", "λίπαρος", "λεῖος", "γλίσχρος", "τραχύ", "ἁλμυρόν", "αὐχμῆσαν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "83e-84b"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 83e-84b
      start_marker: 83e
      end_marker: 84b
      start_char: 130074
      end_char: 131248
      text_sha256: "627ba5ab7ce230abcb0027f80abced95443bb9bfea07630f56d1831bb197282f"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record describes a physiological disease-pathway claim about flesh-to-bone binding failure; it does not establish a general definition of disease or a method rule."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0225
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "84b-84c"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 84b-84c
  start_marker: 84b
  end_marker: 84c
  start_char: 130865
  end_char: 131660
  text_sha256: "113cb007ea8e0439b2ad73b42e18a7c66a75ab88217176587e7c675d8c17a044"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When bone, due to the density of surrounding flesh, does not receive sufficient ventilation, it becomes heated by decay, gangrenes, refuses nourishment, and then the bone matter moves back into the nourishment, which falls into flesh, and flesh into blood, producing diseases harsher than all the previous ones; and when the marrow sickens from deficiency or excess, it produces the greatest and most fatal diseases, with the whole bodily constitution flowing in reverse."
greek_terms: ["σφακελίσαν", "εὐρώς", "μυελός", "ἀνάπαλιν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "84b-84c"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 84b-84c
      start_marker: 84b
      end_marker: 84c
      start_char: 130865
      end_char: 131660
      text_sha256: "113cb007ea8e0439b2ad73b42e18a7c66a75ab88217176587e7c675d8c17a044"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This record covers the disease cascade from bone gangrene to marrow disease; it does not assert a general definition of disease or a single cause applicable to all illness."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0226
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "84c-84d"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 84c-84d

  text_sha256: "967875538a234334ed3c36dc14b0294ae7248bad4579bf68dc1df5c4b82489dc"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "A third class of diseases arises from breath, phlegm, and bile; when the lung, the steward of breaths in the body, is blocked by fluxes so that passages are not clean, breath enters some parts in excess and fails to reach others, causing the unaired parts to rot while the over-aired parts are violently strained."
greek_terms: ["πνεῦμα", "φλέγμα", "χολή", "πλεύμων", "ταμίας"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "84c-84d"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 84c-84d
      start_marker: 84c
      end_marker: 84d

      end_char: 132046
      text_sha256: "967875538a234334ed3c36dc14b0294ae7248bad4579bf68dc1df5c4b82489dc"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This introduces a tripartite classification of diseases by causal substance; it does not claim that all diseases reduce to exactly three causes or that these three exhaust the domain."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0227
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "84e-85a"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 84e-85a
  start_marker: 84e
  end_marker: 85a
  start_char: 132046
  end_char: 133005
  text_sha256: "be20eb1f7c057afd76012871bb012851c595292c3e9eae155c72a81a44c2e6f1"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When breath generated inside the body from decomposed flesh cannot exit, it causes the same pains as incoming breath, and when it accumulates around the sinews and the small veins there and swells, it stretches the tendons and connected sinews backward with great tension; these diseases are called tetanus and opisthotonos, and their remedy is difficult, but fevers supervening upon them are the chief means of release."
greek_terms: ["τέτανοι", "ὀπισθότονοι", "πυρετοί"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "84e-85a"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 84e-85a
      start_marker: 84e

      end_char: 133005
      text_sha256: "be20eb1f7c057afd76012871bb012851c595292c3e9eae155c72a81a44c2e6f1"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This records a causal claim about internal breath causing tetanus-type diseases and fever as remedy; it does not define disease generally or offer a method rule."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0228
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "85a-85b"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

claim_kind: thesis
content: "When white phlegm mixed with black bile is spread over the most divine circuits in the head and disturbs them, it is milder when it attacks during sleep but harder to shake off when it attacks the waking; this disease, being of a sacred nature, is most justly called the sacred disease."
greek_terms: ["φλέγμα", "χολῆς μελαίνης", "περιόδους θειοτάτας", "νόσημα ἱερόν"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "85a-85b"

      stephanus_span: 85a-85b

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This identifies a causal mechanism for the 'sacred disease' (epilepsy); it does not claim that the disease is literally divine in origin — the 'sacred' epithet is etymologically justified by the affected bodily region (the divine circuits in the head)."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0229
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "85b-85c"

content: "Sharp and salty phlegm is the source of all catarrhal diseases, which take on manifold names according to the manifold places into which it flows; and all bodily inflammations arise from bile, which when it finds ventilation outward sends up all sorts of boils while boiling, and when confined within produces many feverish internal diseases."
greek_terms: ["φλέγμα ὀξὺ καὶ ἁλμυρόν", "καταρροϊκά", "φλεγμαίνειν", "κάεσθαι", "φλέγεσθαι", "χολή"]

final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This links catarrhal diseases to phlegm and inflammations to bile causally; it does not claim these exhaust all disease types or constitute a definition of disease."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The greatest disease arises when bile mixed with pure blood disrupts the class of fibers from their proper arrangement; these fibers are dispersed in the blood to maintain due measure of thinness and thickness, preventing the blood from either flowing out as liquid through a porous body or being too dense and sluggish in the veins; bile, being old blood in its nature and melted back from the flesh into the blood, hot and moist, when it enters little by little is first congealed by the power of the fibers, and in being congealed and forcibly quenched produces internal chill and trembling."
greek_terms: ["ἶνες", "χολή", "παλαιὸν αἷμα", "χειμῶνα", "τρόμον"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "85c-85e"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 85c-85e
      start_marker: 85c
      end_marker: 85e
      start_char: 133395
      end_char: 134738
      text_sha256: "7633bbe9ba26c92141fb624a98ea74118bbd9beefc66572f8c9795b3db3ae107"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This records a specific causal pathway for the most serious bile-related disease; it does not define disease in general or assert that all diseases arise from bile-fiber interaction."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0231
source_work: "Timaeus"
stephanus_span: "85e"
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 85e
  start_marker: 85e
  end_marker: 85e
  start_char: 134188
  end_char: 134738
  text_sha256: "ce9530136d13c6353b8142ddc7f418f6d7863c0402185bfc1cdc8613438640bf"
speaker: "ΤΙ."
claim_kind: thesis
content: "When bile flows in more abundantly and overpowers the fibers by its own heat, boiling them into disorder and shaking them apart, if it proves strong enough to prevail completely, it penetrates to the marrow and burns it, loosing the soul's moorings as though from a ship and setting the soul free; if it is weaker and the melting body resists, the bile itself is overpowered and either is expelled over the whole body or driven down through the veins into the lower or upper cavity, like an exile from a city in civil strife."
greek_terms: ["ἀταξίαν", "μυελός", "νεὼς πείσματα", "φυγάς", "πόλεως στασιασάσης"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: "85e"
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 85e
      start_marker: 85e
      end_marker: 85e
      start_char: 134188
      end_char: 134738
      text_sha256: "ce9530136d13c6353b8142ddc7f418f6d7863c0402185bfc1cdc8613438640bf"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "This records a disease-outcome claim about bile overwhelming the marrow and causing death (soul departure) or being expelled; it does not define death or the soul's relation to the body in general, though the 'moorings' simile is notable."
review_status: accepted
```






```yaml

greek_terms: ["μουσικῇ", "φιλοσοφίᾳ", "γυμναστικῇ", "καλός", "ἀγαθὸς"]
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0244
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 88d-88e

content: "The body should imitate the form of the universe by never being kept at rest but by being given internal motions and shakings throughout, so as to defend against incoming and outgoing motions according to nature and to arrange bodily affections and parts into orderly kinship with each other."
greek_terms: ["τὸ τοῦ παντὸς ἀπομιμούμενον εἶδος", "σεισμοὺς", "κατὰ φύσιν κινήσεις", "κατὰ συγγενείας εἰς τάξιν"]

final_status: left_standing

limits: "Asserts a therapeutic method for the body modeled on cosmology; does not establish that all diseases yield to this treatment or that the analogy between bodily and cosmic motion is exact."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

```

```yaml

claim_kind: thesis
content: "For purifications and constitutions of the body, the best kind is through gymnastic exercises, second is through swaying motions such as voyages or any fatigue-free conveyances, and the third kind — the medicinal purification through drugs — is never to be accepted by anyone with intelligence except when absolutely compelled."
greek_terms: ["καθάρσεων", "γυμνασίων", "αἰωρήσεων", "φαρμακευτικῆς καθάρσεως"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted

review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Diseases that do not carry great dangers should not be provoked by drugs, because the constitution of diseases resembles the nature of living creatures in having an allotted lifespan determined by the durability of its constituent triangles; to destroy the disease's constitution prematurely by drugs typically turns small illnesses into many large ones."
greek_terms: ["τρίγωνα", "εἱμαρμένην", "σύστασις νόσων"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 89b-89c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 89b-89c
      start_marker: 89b
      end_marker: 89c
      start_char: 141288
      end_char: 142171
      text_sha256: "f0654da68515706f6571cd7145a2059533c99e0bf993cc161cbf790cfefecc97"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Applies the triangle theory of disease constitution earlier developed in the Timaeus; does not demonstrate this from empirical evidence or distinguish which diseases may safely be treated pharmacologically."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0249
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 89e-90a
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
  stephanus_span: 89e-90a

  end_char: 143462
  text_sha256: "dc96531be0175c855e1a70af9f8ec68d7ca66f0636f720d157f65a5116f5bc90"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "The three kinds of soul housed in us each have their own motions, and whichever kind remains idle and at rest with respect to its own motions necessarily becomes weakest, while whichever is exercised becomes strongest; therefore one must guard that their motions are proportionate to one another."
greek_terms: ["τρία τριχῇ ψυχῆς ἐν ἡμῖν εἴδη", "κινήσεις", "συμμέτρους"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 89e-90a
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 89e-90a
      start_marker: 89e
      end_marker: 90a
      start_char: 142560
      end_char: 143462
      text_sha256: "dc96531be0175c855e1a70af9f8ec68d7ca66f0636f720d157f65a5116f5bc90"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Restates the tripartite soul doctrine and applies a use-it-or-lose-it principle; does not specify what counts as proportionate motion or how to measure imbalance among the soul parts."
review_status: accepted
```

```yaml

greek_terms: ["δαίμονα", "κυριωτάτου παρ' ἡμῖν ψυχῆς εἴδους", "φυτὸν οὐκ ἔγγειον ἀλλὰ οὐράνιον", "κεφαλὴν καὶ ῥίζαν"]
```

```yaml
claim_id: claim_timaeus_0251
source_work: Timaeus
stephanus_span: 90b-90c
source_ref:
  source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt

  end_char: 144300
  text_sha256: "a2ed6641c188c079d63bcdbff89e78c5ddac13a8d10dac8302938f9f8c3f499e"
speaker: ΤΙ.
claim_kind: thesis
content: "Whoever has devoted himself to appetites or ambition and works strenuously at these things must necessarily hold only mortal beliefs and become as mortal as possible, whereas whoever has been earnest about love of learning and true thoughts and has exercised these faculties will necessarily grasp immortal and divine things — insofar as human nature can share in immortality — and will be surpassingly happy, since he always cultivates the divine element and keeps the daimon dwelling within him well ordered."
greek_terms: ["θνητὰ", "ἀθάνατα καὶ θεῖα", "φιλομαθίαν", "ἀληθεῖς φρονήσεις", "δαίμονα σύνοικον", "εὐδαίμονα"]
stance_events:
  - kind: asserted
    stephanus_span: 90b-90c
    source_ref:
      source_path: raw/plato/greek/timaeus.txt
      stephanus_span: 90b-90c
      start_marker: 90b
      end_marker: 90c
      start_char: 143462
      end_char: 144300
      text_sha256: "a2ed6641c188c079d63bcdbff89e78c5ddac13a8d10dac8302938f9f8c3f499e"
final_status: left_standing
observation_ids: []
limits: "Asserts a normative hierarchy of human pursuits with soteriological consequences; does not specify what true thoughts are or how love of learning produces immortality; the claim is conditional on grasping truth."
review_status: accepted
```
